# Abstract

We report mean drag coefficients for smooth circular cylinders of diameter
D = 25.4, 38.1 and 50.8 mm. Each cylinder pierces the free surface with a
submerged length of 0.60 m (L/D = 11.8â€“23.6) and was towed at 0.2â€“1.1 m sâ»Â¹,
giving Reynolds numbers Re = 4.7Ã—10Â³â€“5.0Ã—10â´ and diameter-based Froude numbers
Fr_D = 0.28â€“2.2. Forces were measured with a calibrated in-line load cell and
averaged over a 5 s steady-towing window. Across the 29 accepted runs, C_D =
1.151 Â± 0.030 (mean Â± s.d.). C_D increases weakly with Reynolds number
(0.058 Â± 0.019 per decade). At matched Re, no dependence on diameter is
detectable. The run-to-run scatter (â‰ˆ2.3 %) is more than twice the estimated
per-run measurement uncertainty (â‰ˆ1.1 %), so repeatability, not instrumentation,
limits the precision of the data set. Solid-plus-wake blockage is 2.3â€“4.5 %. Two
standard corrections for it lower C_D by 4â€“12 % and introduce an apparent
decrease of C_D with diameter, so the choice of blockage correction affects the
conclusions. [AUTHOR: one sentence on why this data set matters / who will use it.]

**Keywords:** circular cylinder; drag coefficient; towing tank; free surface;
blockage; bluff-body flow

# 1 Introduction

Steady flow past a circular cylinder is the canonical bluff-body problem. For a
nominally two-dimensional, smooth cylinder in the subcritical regime
(roughly 10Â³ < Re < 2Ã—10âµ), the mean drag coefficient is close to 1.0â€“1.2 and
varies only weakly with Re (Zdravkovich 1997 [verify]; Roshko 1961 [verify]).
The wake sheds vortices at a Strouhal number St â‰ˆ 0.2 (Williamson 1996 [verify];
Norberg 2003 [verify]).

Many practical cylinders are neither two-dimensional nor fully submerged.
Examples include piles, risers, instrument masts and the struts used in
laboratory towing experiments. A vertical cylinder that pierces the free
surface and has a free end below it is affected by three things that the
canonical problem excludes:

1. a free-end (tip) flow that lowers sectional drag near the tip, so that
   finite-length cylinders have lower drag than 2D ones (Fox and West 1993 [verify]);
2. free-surface deformation and wave generation, which depend on the Froude
   number and can change the near-surface wake (Chaplin and Teigen 2003 [verify]);
3. confinement by the tank walls and floor (blockage), which raises the
   measured drag (Maskell 1963 [verify]; West and Apelt 1982 [verify]).

[AUTHOR: state the gap this work fills and the purpose of the measurements,
e.g. a reference data set for a teaching/benchmark facility, validation data for
a numerical study, or characterisation of the tank. The data alone do not show
which of these it is.]

This paper reports drag measurements on three diameters at a fixed submerged
length. That allows partial separation of Reynolds-number and diameter effects.
Particular attention goes to the data-reduction choices (fluid properties,
steady window, run rejection, blockage) that change the reported C_D by amounts
comparable to the effects of interest.

# 2 Experimental method

## 2.1 Facility and models

Experiments were performed in a towing tank with a rectangular cross-section
0.90 m wide and 0.75 m deep. [AUTHOR: tank length, carriage drive and how
carriage speed is set/verified, settling time between runs, water
treatment/filtration.] Three smooth circular cylinders of nominal diameter
25.4, 38.1 and 50.8 mm (1, 1.5 and 2 in) were tested. [AUTHOR: material,
surface finish/roughness, manufacturing tolerance on D, tip geometry (flat?
end plate?).] Each cylinder was mounted vertically from the carriage, piercing
the free surface, with a submerged length L = 0.60 m. That leaves a gap of
0.15 m between the free end and the tank floor (2.95â€“5.9 D).
[AUTHOR: confirm L = 0.60 m and how it was set/measured. See Section 2.3: an
earlier analysis that used L = 0.70 m gave C_D values 14 % lower.] Table 1
lists the geometric parameters.

**Table 1.** Model geometry and test conditions. Blockage ratio
B = DL/(WH), with W and H the tank width and water depth.

| D (mm) | L/D | B (%) | U (m sâ»Â¹) | Water T (Â°C) | Re (Ã—10Â³) | Fr_D | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25.4 | 23.6 | 2.26 | 0.2â€“1.1 | 16.9â€“17.6 | 4.7â€“26.3 | 0.40â€“2.20 | 10 |
| 38.1 | 15.7 | 3.39 | 0.2â€“1.1 | 13.7â€“14.3 | 6.5â€“36.2 | 0.33â€“1.80 | 10 |
| 50.8 | 11.8 | 4.52 | 0.2â€“1.1 | 15.0â€“15.6 | 8.9â€“49.9 | 0.28â€“1.56 | 9 (+1 rejected) |

[AUTHOR: check that "water depth" H = 0.75 m is the water depth, not the
tank wall height. B and the blockage corrections scale with it.]

## 2.2 Force measurement and calibration

The streamwise force on the cylinder was measured with a load cell
[AUTHOR: make/model, rated capacity, mounting arrangement, whether it senses
force only or also moment, cross-axis sensitivity]. The analogue output was
recorded at 200 Hz for 12 s per run. [AUTHOR: DAQ hardware, anti-alias
filtering, if any.]

The load cell was calibrated statically by hanging masses of 0â€“2000 g in
loading and unloading sequences (nine points). A linear least-squares fit gave a
sensitivity of 0.02001 V Nâ»Â¹ for the September 2025 calibration, which applies
to the 25.4 and 50.8 mm cylinders. After the load cell was remounted in March
2026, recalibration gave 0.02129 V Nâ»Â¹, which applies to the 38.1 mm cylinder.
In both cases the standard error of the slope is below 0.1 % and the largest
residual is below 17 mN, with no detectable loading/unloading hysteresis. The
6.4 % change in sensitivity after remounting shows why the instrument must be
recalibrated whenever it is remounted. [AUTHOR: was the September calibration
re-checked before the November runs? If not, say so here. See Section 4.3.]

## 2.3 Test procedure and data reduction

Each run consisted of a â‰ˆ1.5 s stationary period, acceleration to the target
speed, steady towing, deceleration and a final stationary period (Fig. 1).
Load-cell voltages were converted to force with

  F(t) = [V(t) âˆ’ VÌ„â‚€] / S,

where S is the calibration sensitivity and VÌ„â‚€ is the mean voltage over the
first 1 s of the record (carriage at rest). The mean drag FÌ„ was taken as the
average over 3 s â‰¤ t â‰¤ 8 s. In every run the force first reaches 90 % of its
steady value at t â‰ˆ 2.4â€“2.5 s and leaves the plateau at t â‰ˆ 9.0â€“9.1 s, so the
averaging window lies entirely within steady towing, with â‰¥0.5 s margin at
each end. At the highest speed the window corresponds to â‰ˆ 110 diameters of
travel for the 50.8 mm cylinder. [AUTHOR: confirm the steady-speed duration
from the carriage controller.]

The drag coefficient, Reynolds number and Froude number are

  C_D = FÌ„ / (Â½ Ï UÂ² D L),  Re = U D / Î½,  Fr_D = U / âˆš(gD),

with U the nominal carriage speed. The water density Ï and kinematic viscosity
Î½ were evaluated at the water temperature logged for each run, using the
density correlation of Tanaka et al. (2001) [verify] and the Vogel equation for
dynamic viscosity. The water was 13.7â€“17.6 Â°C. Assuming 20 Â°C instead would
overstate Re by 6â€“17 %. The effect on Ï, and hence on C_D, is <0.2 %.

**Run rejection.** The zero offset was also measured over the last 1 s of
each record (carriage at rest after the run). For 29 of the 30 runs, the
pre- and post-run zeros agree within 3.5 mN. For run 17 (D = 50.8 mm,
U = 0.8 m sâ»Â¹), the post-run offset had shifted by 4.2 N, about 30 % of the
mean drag. The force record shows a steady ramp-like drift that begins during
acceleration (not shown). This run was rejected (criterion: |zero shift| >
0.05 N). Had it been included it would give C_D = 1.36, â‰ˆ18 % above its
neighbours. [AUTHOR: any known cause, e.g. cable snag, thermal drift,
connector? Was a repeat run attempted?]

**Uncertainty.** The per-run standard uncertainty of C_D was estimated by
combining the following in quadrature:

- precision of the mean force, from five 1 s block means (â‰ˆ0.1 %);
- the calibration slope (â‰¤0.07 %);
- carriage speed, entering as 2u(U)/U [AUTHOR: actual value, 0.5 % assumed];
- diameter [AUTHOR: actual tolerance, 0.05 mm assumed];
- submerged length [AUTHOR: actual value, 2 mm assumed].

With the assumed values the combined uncertainty is â‰ˆ1.1 %, dominated by
carriage speed. Systematic effects that are not in this budget are discussed
in Section 4.

![**Fig. 1** Force record for the 25.4 mm cylinder at U = 0.6 m sâ»Â¹ (run 5). Shading: averaging window. Dashed line: window mean.](figures/fig1_timeseries_run005.pdf)

# 3 Results

## 3.1 Mean drag coefficient

Figure 2 shows C_D against Re for all runs. Table 2 summarises the results for
each cylinder. The three diameters overlap in Re over the range
â‰ˆ 9Ã—10Â³â€“2.6Ã—10â´, and in that range their C_D values cannot be distinguished.
Across all 29 accepted runs,

  C_D = 1.151 Â± 0.030 (mean Â± s.d.).

**Table 2.** Drag coefficient by cylinder (run 17 excluded). The last two
columns show the effect of the blockage corrections in Section 4.2.

| D (mm) | n | C_D mean | C_D s.d. | Câ€²_D (r.m.s.) | C_D, (1âˆ’B)Â² | C_D, Maskell Î¸ = 2.5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25.4 | 10 | 1.143 | 0.033 | 0.056 | 1.092 | 1.074 |
| 38.1 | 10 | 1.152 | 0.026 | 0.055 | 1.076 | 1.050 |
| 50.8 | 9 | 1.157 | 0.034 | 0.054 | 1.055 | 1.024 |

A linear least-squares fit of C_D to logâ‚â‚€Re over all runs gives a slope of
0.058 Â± 0.019 per decade (correlation coefficient r = 0.50). Adding D as a
second regressor gives a diameter coefficient of âˆ’0.001 Â± 0.014 per 25.4 mm,
which is not distinguishable from zero. Its inclusion leaves the Re slope
unchanged (0.059 Â± 0.022). The scatter about this fit is 0.027 in C_D
(2.3 %). That is about twice the estimated per-run uncertainty (error bars in
Fig. 2). Adjacent runs on the same cylinder sometimes differ by 4â€“8 %, e.g.
runs 2 and 3 (25.4 mm) and runs 18 and 19 (50.8 mm).

![**Fig. 2** Drag coefficient against Reynolds number. Error bars: estimated per-run standard uncertainty (Section 2.3). Ã—: rejected run 17.](figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.pdf)

## 3.2 Unsteady drag and shedding frequency

The steady-window force records show a periodic fluctuation (Fig. 1) with an
r.m.s. of Câ€²_D â‰ˆ 0.05â€“0.07, i.e. 4â€“7 % of the mean. The dominant frequency of
the drag fluctuation is f_D. Taking the shedding frequency as f_s = f_D/2, the
Strouhal number St = f_s D/U = 0.200 for every run, to within Â±0.0003
(Fig. 3a).

> [AUTHOR â€” please check this before keeping Section 3.2.] A Strouhal number
> constant to Â±0.15 % across 30 runs, three diameters, two calibrations and a
> 10Ã— range of Re (including the rejected run 17) is much tighter than
> published shedding measurements, which typically scatter by several percent
> and vary slightly with Re. Ratios Câ€²_D/C_D are also nearly constant. This
> pattern would be expected if the raw files contained a synthetic or
> injected signal rather than measured wake forcing. Please confirm the
> provenance of the raw data (e.g. that no test/simulated files from DAQ
> development are among them). If the data are real, this section needs an
> explanation for the narrowness, e.g. a structural resonance locking the
> response.

![**Fig. 3** (a) Strouhal number from the dominant drag-fluctuation frequency, assuming f_D = 2f_s. (b) Drag coefficient against diameter-based Froude number.](figures/fig3_st_and_froude.pdf)

## 3.3 Froude-number dependence

Because all three cylinders were towed at the same speeds, the Froude number
Fr_D = U/âˆš(gD) covers 0.28â€“2.2 and is partly correlated with Re within each
cylinder. C_D correlates with Fr_D (r = 0.43) slightly less well than with
log Re (r = 0.50) (Fig. 3b). At equal Fr_D the three diameters do not separate
systematically. With a single submerged length and one run per condition, the
present data cannot separate free-surface effects from Reynolds-number effects.
[AUTHOR: any qualitative observations of the free surface, e.g. bow wave,
ventilation behind the cylinder at the higher speeds? Run 4 is logged with
"small wave from previous run"; its C_D is not anomalous.]

# 4 Discussion

## 4.1 Comparison with two-dimensional data

The measured C_D â‰ˆ 1.15 lies at the upper end of the range usually quoted for
smooth 2D cylinders at 10â´ â‰² Re â‰² 5Ã—10â´ (â‰ˆ1.0â€“1.2; Zdravkovich 1997 [verify]).
That is a notable result for cylinders with a free end and L/D = 12â€“24,
because tip effects alone are expected to *reduce* the mean drag below the 2D
value (Fox and West 1993 [verify]). Possible explanations, which the present
data cannot distinguish, are blockage (Section 4.2), free-surface effects
(Section 3.3), drag on the mounting hardware or on the cylinder above the
waterline, and any difference between nominal and actual carriage speed.
[AUTHOR: insert specific literature values for finite and surface-piercing
cylinders at comparable L/D, Re and Fr for a quantitative comparison.]

## 4.2 Blockage

The blockage ratio B = DL/(WH) is 2.3, 3.4 and 4.5 % for the three cylinders.
Wall-confinement corrections for bluff bodies were derived for closed wind-tunnel
test sections. They do not strictly apply to a tank with a free surface and a
partly spanning model, and no correction is applied to the headline values.
Two common forms bracket the likely size of the effect:

- a simple solid-plus-wake form, C_D,c = C_D (1 âˆ’ B)Â²;
- a Maskell-type form, C_D,c = C_D / (1 + Î¸ C_D B) with Î¸ = 2.5
  (Maskell 1963 [verify]).

They reduce C_D by 4.5â€“8.8 % and 6.0â€“11.5 % respectively (Table 2). Because B
grows with D, either correction turns the diameter-independent raw C_D into
one that *decreases* with D by â‰ˆ0.04â€“0.05 over the range tested. In this
experiment, aspect ratio L/D also falls as D grows. A decrease of C_D with
falling L/D is the expected free-end trend, so the corrected data would be
physically plausible but cannot attribute the trend to either cause. The
blockage treatment therefore changes the qualitative conclusion of the study.
It should be settled before submission, ideally by an additional
experiment (e.g. one diameter at two submerged lengths or two water depths).

## 4.3 Sources of systematic error and repeatability

- **Repeatability.** Each condition was run once, and the scatter between
  neighbouring conditions (â‰ˆ2.3 %) exceeds the estimated instrumental
  uncertainty. Repeated runs would quantify an uncertainty component that is
  currently unaccounted for and would let the Re trend in Section 3.1 be
  tested properly.
- **Calibration stability.** The 25.4 mm and 50.8 mm cylinders were tested 2
  and 5 weeks after the September calibration without an intervening check.
  The 6.4 % shift observed after remounting shows that mounting conditions
  matter. [AUTHOR: details of any in-situ check.]
- **Carriage speed.** U is the nominal set-point. Because C_D âˆ Uâ»Â², a 1 %
  speed error gives a 2 % drag-coefficient error.
- **Submerged length.** An early reduction with L = 0.70 m gave C_D â‰ˆ 0.94â€“1.01
  for the 25.4 mm cylinder, exactly 6/7 of the present values. This shows how
  directly C_D depends on the assumed wetted length, including the effect of
  the free-surface elevation change at the cylinder at higher Fr_D.

# 5 Conclusions

[AUTHOR: revise once the blockage treatment and Section 3.2 are settled.]

1. Smooth surface-piercing cylinders with L/D = 12â€“24 towed at
   Re = 4.7Ã—10Â³â€“5.0Ã—10â´ have an uncorrected mean drag coefficient of
   1.151 Â± 0.030, with a weak increase with Re (â‰ˆ0.06 per decade).
2. Without blockage correction, C_D does not depend on diameter at matched
   Re. Standard blockage corrections (B = 2.3â€“4.5 %) lower C_D by 4â€“12 % and
   produce a decrease with diameter that is confounded with aspect ratio.
3. Run-to-run scatter, not instrumental uncertainty, limits the precision of
   the present data. Using the actual water temperature changes Re by up to
   17 %, and a post-run zero check is an effective rejection criterion.

	ë|#´»¬Í4É2\yx¶)ûRº¾âe'S¦arâ<Ş’w¨–ùx¬ÿBµl¿9Ê]d—ÎÜÓĞ$ÛÌm‰ùò*õŒUYsèÑpÖã®¥áÔWjš£‹áæ· FŒÏ¿YˆïÌ»âŠ<tìüU1-MÍÄ‰«T¢j&{¨ÿ;iazdÁôñ²^jCVçÉeQšéšÿ~% ¯m[ø¤ëx3÷°ÃË¯Ë—ëItº^ì=0y&À»Eº[ÉSGû&eæÖò¸½tú;¶¾¾^ª»sšrAhTm[>äÎVÑ£±®ke!ÚSâÅŠı×\¡Ô8¼IçNZË§‰à€òÌ–‚È‘îTlŠs1ès$>IJº)bq]†ı”l$u&mNÎTT‚ïÅàe$Ô‡£‚‚Ë˜ÚV×wZ~™XFÓÃÖÌ•»ƒ{»İI±ŸQFRV	•» J «½j×İßãx€óL³µ2™Ô¹M*0ÎÔ'èÏY¡'#Ö¡øåøñ;ëµ3*:~Pµ+ìäòDÆ(œGÒˆ”ÍÂzß‡a;ÓÚÚ*ØGLe@¨\Mû çæ;yKÔ5KÆƒE›©éZQÑû$+zB ùƒõ,ÑemÏD’¹ª• Š-=ˆ“ƒ,Æä4í''2‘aÊu¹{àÒ8ÈÆ(GÄ]„Öo}ı±$LB¡åßvF¡Yy‡áv]›Ç‚$öâÎæÔ1§WŸãQâ=KªôH¨áò—¼Wöú{À†a±Û}òÄ	hŸºH9âó Tãk9¹Àİ·•Ùõõêrw¶+°öDœ°cs‚ l%º|-~)‡&‰1ùUé÷áÛvªTx,hxbÛ3‰m)zŒİ÷µÁhD,CÊP;8×Ÿ‰3€&BğãvcDÑÏÅ-æº¤¢HÖ8Oº”xé×³n Ôù*ó²(Vtºòg¢*Ìû×7&Rp_Ã-ù–/UB‹V°¨(`9”;kƒãÕk1ÏeĞå3ğHÕDŒÁö%û+}áe(ÖL´ÛŞø¡DÇEÉÚ^Ù«aû*Š¤\º"ùÕÔ”Kyæ¤±^·šR’|ˆ%ôã­¥E@µ #OeİláÕ¬ŞÓË?¾µŒ?’àoáü
ÿôîx‚ë-Ûğ?ÚÈ4×S*Ä¤Ğ‹†ú{×ä„…uó*ÄOW…àŒ[¨	dV\0+âîÊ?4ÿÖO))ÙŞÙ^Ô‚|g&í•>+6³§¹1HÈ”aâ)IÓ‚Ãâb`÷	ƒq&„Šûÿí%*üãåÿß&Ôï›ïî¹t?†ß=ÓWåRi¼Ê!16>ùë½r¿ %ú¿ø—¯é¿üË³ãqˆ›3|©£a¨^pñjğÿ PK   Çr^]dNFd  Ô     docProps/app.xmlRËNÃ0¼÷+¢Ü‰Kyªr]!â ©)œ-{“X8¶eÏnÓ† 8‘ÓîÌÎìf¾şèmñ1ïVåq5/pÊkãÚU¹­o.Ëµ˜ñ§èÄl (piUv9‡%cIuĞËT!íi|ìeÆ6¶Ì7QpãÕ[.³Å|~Îà#ƒÓ ÂhXË÷ü_Síİ—ëÏ€~bVüÅGÄå	gCEØ¦“4jE#mÎ¾¢ïP­q¯éº“®}ûMĞø½qÄñ‚³¡"ì*„ç!K$ª9>œM°½ì5mCíod†Ã†ŸàŞÉ%3ÉŒŠ>ù&ô.9Wƒñ8B<.J•q×‹Éİ&H…WQ2$©¡–V>RÄ¶Ò>÷œ(`:PoÑäOK§íÎÁgikÓƒ8GáØìâVÒÂ5~˜1îøy®8½8›¹£Ÿ°k£~EÎ&İ@¶”=áTÌ°ÿ'ñPK   Çr^]š¬½	  j,     word/theme/theme1.xmlíZMoÛ6¾÷Wº»–dK¶‹º…?›¶I4n‡i™¶S¢@ÒI¢ÀĞv0 vXİv†X»ìÇh±u?b”;¢,Ón:´Æš"’ÏÃ÷}ù~™ÖÕëã˜†uÃºl …àpT7î÷º…ª¸€á ¢º1EÜ¸~íÒUxEø(@@ÂC~Ö_ˆèJ±È=9ùe¡PÎ) lT0x,iR´MÓ-‡a Yï‡ØC S×.0çïù'<KF=Â¼dç4Ò˜Í'+ckş”<ó)o ©rÿ=î¡GÂ r!'ê†™üÅGQ!‘D¬£LÑu“•.EHh«tlÔ_ğ™»Z¶²ÒØŠ4x§ÿfwOÃ¡çI‹Z«),Ç5«¶J‘-ht’Ô*V)—fYš’FššÛ´Ëy4¥%š²Æ¬İZ§íäÑ”—hœÕ4ÓnÖJy4Î»š¦ÜiTìN›¢ñ	Ç·R­º*‰‘€!%;z–šëš•¶Ê¢¢â‘EØ-qHC±&xHYW®Sv'Pàˆi„†Ğ“¸F$(mÌ#§ˆ`H¹6mË’aY6íÅoÊ&S4™9¯‹EÜc8uã–ÜĞH­}óúõÉÓW'O?yöìäé¯`|¡#Øá(Mğî§oşyñ%øû·ß=ÿv§oùêín´¡P$şîåÛW/ß|ÿõ_??×áöÓ¸wĞ1¸Giİ–¨ÏÎ	íù§¡pÄac°Ö¾»3…ê M¤Ã&³qcr¨(uà³‰À:Äm?P{”’&ezÜÅHÛnÖÈÅ&iÀ=´bµ2Ô™D2.±v“–Uö‰ô*8B! £c„tø‡+ç³‡=F9

2in,9,50.8,11.81,4.516,1.157,0.03363,1.055,1.024,0.2001,0.0001608,0.05379,1.072
=== ./paper/paper_analysis.py
# Reproduces every number, table and figure in paper/draft.md from the raw data.
# Run from the project root:  python paper/paper_analysis.py
#
# Differences from process.py / process_b3.py / analysis.ipynb:
#   - one script for both DAQ formats; sensitivity fitted from each calibration file
#   - nu and rho evaluated at the logged water temperature of each run (was 20 C)
#   - runs flagged by a post-run zero-shift criterion are excluded (run 17)
#   - adds uncertainty, Strouhal number, Froude number and a blockage sensitivity check
import glob
import os

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

OUT = "paper"
os.makedirs(f"{OUT}/figures", exist_ok=True)

G = 9.81
L = 0.60                      # submerged length (m)
TANK_W, TANK_H = 0.90, 0.75   # tank cross-section (m)
T_TARE = 1.0                  # tare window: t < 1 s
WIN = (3.0, 8.0)              # steady averaging window (s)
ZERO_SHIFT_MAX = 0.05         # N; exclusion threshold on |post-run zero - pre-run zero|

# assumed (placeholder) standard uncertainties -- CONFIRM WITH AUTHORS
U_REL_SPEED = 0.005   # carriage speed, relative
U_D = 0.05e-3         # diameter, m
U_L = 2e-3            # submerged length, m

CAL = {"2025-09": "data/calibration/loadcell_2025-09.csv",
       "2026-03": "data/calibration/loadcell_2026-03.csv"}
BATCH_CAL = {"2025-10-14": "2025-09", "2025-11-03": "2025-09", "2026-03-12": "2026-03"}


def water_rho(T):
    """Density of pure water (kg/m^3), Tanaka et al. (2001) form."""
    return 999.974950 * (1 - (T - 3.983035) ** 2 * (T + 301.797)
                         / (522528.9 * (T + 69.34881)))


def water_mu(T):
    """Dynamic viscosity of water (Pa s), Vogel equation."""
    return 2.414e-5 * 10 ** (247.8 / (T + 273.15 - 140.0))


def fit_cal(path):
    c = pd.read_csv(path)
    x = c.mass_g / 1000 * G
    p, cov = np.polyfit(x, c.voltage_V, 1, cov=True)
    resid = c.voltage_V - np.polyval(p, x)
    return dict(sens=p[0], sens_se=np.sqrt(cov[0, 0]), offset=p[1],
                max_resid_N=resid.abs().max() / p[0], n=len(c))


def read_run(path):
    if "2026-03" in path:
        d = pd.read_csv(path, sep=";")
        return d.t.values, d.V.values
    d = pd.read_csv(path, comment="#")
    return d.time_s.values, d.loadcell_V.values


def shedding_freq(F, fs):
    """Dominant drag-fluctuation frequency (Hann window, 16x zero-padding)."""
    x = (F - F.mean()) * np.hanning(len(F))
    n = 16 * len(x)
    spec = np.abs(np.fft.rfft(x, n))
    f = np.fft.rfftfreq(n, 1 / fs)
    sel = f > 0.5
    return f[sel][np.argmax(spec[sel])]


cals = {k: fit_cal(v) for k, v in CAL.items()}
log = pd.read_csv("data/run_log.csv")

rows = []
for path in sorted(glob.glob("data/raw/*/run_*.csv")):
    batch = path.split(os.sep)[-2]
    cal = cals[BATCH_CAL[batch]]
    t, v = read_run(path)
    fs = 1 / np.median(np.diff(t))
    zero_pre = v[t < T_TARE].mean()
    zero_post = v[t > t.max() - T_TARE].mean()
    F = (v - zero_pre) / cal["sens"]
    w = (t > WIN[0]) & (t < WIN[1])
    Fw = F[w]
    # precision of the mean from five 1-s block means
    blocks = np.array_split(Fw, 5)
    bm = np.array([b.mean() for b in blocks])
    rows.append(dict(run=int(os.path.basename(path)[4:7]), batch=batch,
                     F=Fw.mean(), F_rms=Fw.std(), F_se=bm.std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(len(bm)),
                     sens=cal["sens"], sens_rel=cal["sens_se"] / cal["sens"],
                     zero_shift_N=(zero_post - zero_pre) / cal["sens"],
                     f_drag=shedding_freq(Fw, fs)))

df = pd.DataFrame(rows).merge(log, left_on="run", right_on="run_id")
D = df.diameter_mm / 1000
U = df.speed_m_s
T = df.water_temp_c
df["rho"] = water_rho(T)
df["nu"] = water_mu(T) / df.rho
df["Re"] = U * D / df.nu
df["Re_20C"] = U * D / 1.004e-6
df["Fr_D"] = U / np.sqrt(G * D)
df["LD"] = L / D
q = 0.5 * df.rho * U ** 2 * D * L
df["Cd"] = df.F / q
df["Cd_rms"] = df.F_rms / q
df["St"] = (df.f_drag / 2) * D / U         # drag fluctuates at twice the shedding frequency
df["blockage"] = D * L / (TANK_W * TANK_H)
# blockage sensitivity: Maskell-type correction, theta = 2.5 (upper) and simple (1-B)^2 (lower)
df["Cd_maskell"] = df.Cd / (1 + 2.5 * df.Cd * df.blockage)
df["Cd_simple"] = df.Cd * (1 - df.blockage) ** 2
# uncertainty in Cd (root-sum-square of relative terms)
df["u_Cd_rel"] = np.sqrt((df.F_se / df.F) ** 2 + df.sens_rel ** 2 + (2 * U_REL_SPEED) ** 2
                         + (U_D / D) ** 2 + (U_L / L) ** 2)
df["u_Cd"] = df.u_Cd_rel * df.Cd
df["excluded"] = df.zero_shift_N.abs() > ZERO_SHIFT_MAX

good = df[~df.excluded]
order = ["1in", "1.5in", "2in"]

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- printed summary
print("Calibration")
for k, c in cals.items():
    print(f"  {k}: sens = {c['sens']:.5f} V/N (SE {c['sens_se']/c['sens']*100:.2f}%), "
          f"max residual {c['max_resid_N']*1000:.1f} mN, n = {c['n']}")
print(f"  sensitivity change between calibrations: "
      f"{(cals['2026-03']['sens']/cals['2025-09']['sens']-1)*100:+.1f}%")

print("\nZero shift (N): max |shift| among retained runs = "
      f"{good.zero_shift_N.abs().max():.4f}; excluded runs:")
print(df.loc[df.excluded, ["run", "cylinder", "speed_m_s", "zero_shift_N", "Cd"]].round(4).to_string(index=False))

print("\nRe range per cylinder (temperature-corrected) and ratio to 20 C value")
print(good.groupby("cylinder").agg(Re_min=("Re", "min"), Re_max=("Re", "max"),
      T_mean=("water_temp_c", "mean"), Re20_over_Re=("Re_20C", lambda s: 0)).loc[order].round(0))
print("  Re_20C/Re ratio range:", (good.Re_20C / good.Re).min().round(3), "-", (good.Re_20C / good.Re).max().round(3))

summ = good.groupby("cylinder").agg(
    n=("Cd", "size"), D_mm=("diameter_mm", "first"), LD=("LD", "first"),
    B_pct=("blockage", lambda s: 100 * s.iloc[0]),
    Cd_mean=("Cd", "mean"), Cd_sd=("Cd", "std"),
    Cd_simple=("Cd_simple", "mean"), Cd_maskell=("Cd_maskell", "mean"),
    St_mean=("St", "mean"), St_sd=("St", "std"), Cdrms_mean=("Cd_rms", "mean"),
    u_rel_pct=("u_Cd_rel", lambda s: 100 * s.mean())).loc[order]
print("\nPer-cylinder summary (run 17 excluded)")
print(summ.round(3).to_string())
print(f"\nAll retained runs: n = {len(good)}, Cd = {good.Cd.mean():.3f} +/- {good.Cd.std():.3f} (sd), "
      f"St = {good.St.mean():.3f} +/- {good.St.std():.3f}")
print("Notebook values incl. run 17, 20 C:  2in mean Cd =",
      round(df[df.cylinder == '2in'].Cd.mean(), 3))

# trend tests
for name, x in [("log10 Re", np.log10(good.Re)), ("Fr_D", good.Fr_D)]:
    p, cov = np.polyfit(x, good.Cd, 1, cov=True)
    r = np.corrcoef(x, good.Cd)[0, 1]
    print(f"Cd vs {name}: slope {p[0]:.3f} +/- {np.sqrt(cov[0,0]):.3f}, r = {r:.2f}")
# does Cd depend on D at matched Re?  Compare at shared Re values via a regression Cd ~ logRe + D
X = np.column_stack([np.ones(len(good)), np.log10(good.Re), good.diameter_mm])
beta, res, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(X, good.Cd, rcond=None)
sig2 = res[0] / (len(good) - 3)
se = np.sqrt(np.diag(sig2 * np.linalg.inv(X.T @ X)))
print(f"Cd ~ a + b log10Re + c D[mm]: b = {beta[1]:.3f} +/- {se[1]:.3f}, c = {beta[2]*25.4:.3f} +/- {se[2]*25.4:.3f} per inch")
print(f"Run-to-run residual sd about the 2-term fit: {np.sqrt(sig2):.3f}")
print(f"Mean estimated u(Cd)/Cd: {good.u_Cd_rel.mean()*100:.2f}%  "
      f"(force-mean precision alone: {(good.F_se/good.F).mean()*100:.2f}%)")
print("Fr_D range:", good.Fr_D.min().round(2), "-", good.Fr_D.max().round(2))
print("Cd_rms/Cd range:", (good.Cd_rms / good.Cd).min().round(3), "-", (good.Cd_rms / good.Cd).max().round(3))

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- tables
tab = df[["run", "cylinder", "diameter_mm", "speed_m_s", "water_temp_c", "Re", "Fr_D",
          "F", "Cd", "u_Cd", "Cd_rms", "St", "zero_shift_N", "excluded"]].copy()
tab.to_csv(f"{OUT}/results_by_run.csv", index=False, float_format="%.5g")
summ.to_csv(f"{OUT}/results_by_cylinder.csv", float_format="%.4g")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- figures
COL = {"1in": "#2a78d6", "1.5in": "#eb6834", "2in": "#1baf7a"}
MRK = {"1in": "o", "1.5in": "s", "2in": "^"}
LAB = {"1in": "D = 25.4 mm", "1.5in": "D = 38.1 mm", "2in": "D = 50.8 mm"}
plt.rcParams.update({"font.size": 9, "axes.spines.top": False, "axes.spines.right": False,
                     "axes.edgecolor": "#555", "axes.labelcolor": "#222",
                     "xtick.color": "#555", "ytick.color": "#555"})


def style(ax):
    ax.grid(True, which="major", color="#e6e6e6", lw=0.6)
    ax.set_axisbelow(True)


# Fig 2: Cd vs Re
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5.0, 3.4))
for cyl in order:
    g = good[good.cylinder == cyl]
    ax.errorbar(g.Re, g.Cd, yerr=g.u_Cd, fmt=MRK[cyl], ms=5, color=COL[cyl], mec="white",
                mew=0.6, elinewidth=0.8, capsize=0, label=LAB[cyl])
ex = df[df.excluded]
ax.plot(ex.Re, ex.Cd, "x", color="#888", ms=6, label="run 17 (excluded)")
ax.set_xscale("log")
ax.set_xlabel("Re = UD/Î½")
ax.set_ylabel("$C_D$")
style(ax)
ax.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8, loc="upper left")
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.png", dpi=300)
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig2_cd_vs_re.pdf")

# Fig 1: example force record
t, v = read_run("data/raw/2025-10-14/run_005.csv")
F = (v - v[t < T_TARE].mean()) / cals["2025-09"]["sens"]
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(5.0, 2.6))
ax.axvspan(*WIN, color="#eeeeee", lw=0)
ax.plot(t, F, lw=0.5, color=COL["1in"])
ax.axhline(F[(t > WIN[0]) & (t < WIN[1])].mean(), color="#222", lw=0.8, ls="--")
ax.text(5.5, 0.4, "averaging window", ha="center", fontsize=8, color="#555")
ax.set_xlabel("t (s)")
ax.set_ylabel("F (N)")
style(ax)
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig1_timeseries_run005.png", dpi=300)
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig1_timeseries_run005.pdf")

# Fig 3: Strouhal number and Cd vs Froude number (two panels, separate y axes)
fig, axs = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(7.0, 2.9))
for cyl in order:
    g = good[good.cylinder == cyl]
    axs[0].plot(g.Re, g.St, MRK[cyl], ms=5, color=COL[cyl], mec="white", mew=0.6, label=LAB[cyl])
    axs[1].plot(g.Fr_D, g.Cd, MRK[cyl], ms=5, color=COL[cyl], mec="white", mew=0.6, label=LAB[cyl])

for a, lbl in zip(axs, "ab"):
    style(a)
    a.text(-0.2, 1.02, f"({lbl})", transform=a.transAxes, fontsize=9)
axs[1].legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8)
fig.tight_layout()
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig3_st_and_froude.png", dpi=300)
fig.savefig(f"{OUT}/figures/fig3_st_and_froude.pdf")
print("\nwrote", OUT)
=== ./old_matlab/towtank_proc.m
% towtank_proc.m  - J. Rivera, 2024
% mean drag from one run file. usage: F = towtank_proc('run_001.csv', 0.02)
function F = towtank_proc(fname, sens)
    d = readmatrix(fname, 'NumHeaderLines', 3);
    t = d(:,1); v = d(:,2);
    tare = mean(v(t < 1));
    f = (v - tare) / sens;
    F = mean(f(t > 3 & t < 8));
end

=== ./README.md
# towtank-drag

Drag on smooth circular cylinders towed through the tank. 1in, 2in and
1.5in cylinders, 0.6 m submerged.

## Processing

1. `python process.py` (change DATA path to wherever the raw data is)
2. `python process_b3.py` for the March data (new DAQ format)
3. run `analysis.ipynb` -> Cd vs Re plot in figures/

`plot_timeseries.py` makes the example trace figure.

Calibration files are in data/calibration. Run log is data/run_log.csv.

old_matlab/ is Jamie's original processing script, kept for reference.
=== ./plot_timeseries.py
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import pandas as pd

d = pd.read_csv("data/raw/2025-10-14/run_005.csv", comment="#")
sens = 0.0200
F = (d.loadcell_V - d.loadcell_V[d.time_s < 1].mean()) / sens

plt.figure(figsize=(6, 3))
plt.plot(d.time_s, F, lw=0.5)
plt.axvspan(3, 8, color="gray", alpha=0.2)
plt.xlabel("t (s)")
plt.ylabel("F (N)")
plt.title("run 5")
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig("figures/timeseries_run005.png", dpi=150)
=== ./process.py
# process tow tank runs -> mean drag force for each run
import glob
import os

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

DATA = "/Users/mkim/Dropbox/towtank/data/raw"
BATCHES = ["2025-10-14", "2025-11-03"]

cal = pd.read_csv("data/calibration/loadcell_2025-09.csv")
sens = np.polyfit(cal.mass_g / 1000 * 9.81, cal.voltage_V, 1)[0]  # V/N
print("sensitivity", sens)

rows = []
for b in BATCHES:
    for f in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA, b, "run_*.csv"))):
        d = pd.read_csv(f, comment="#")
        tare = d.loadcell_V[d.time_s < 1.0].mean()
        F = (d.loadcell_V - tare) / sens
        steady = (d.time_s > 3) & (d.time_s < 8)
        rows.append({
            "run": int(os.path.basename(f)[4:7]),
            "F_mean": F[steady].mean(),
            "F_std": F[steady].std(),
        })

out = pd.DataFrame(rows)
out.to_csv("processed/forces.csv", index=False)
print("done", len(out))
=== ./process_b3.py
# same as process.py but for the march runs (new DAQ software, ; separated)
# TODO merge into process.py
import glob
import os

import pandas as pd

SENS = 0.0213  # V/N, from march cal

rows = []
for f in sorted(glob.glob("data/raw/2026-03-12/run_*.csv")):
    d = pd.read_csv(f, sep=";")
    tare = d.V[d.t < 1.0].mean()
    F = (d.V - tare) / SENS
    steady = (d.t > 3) & (d.t < 8)
    rows.append({
        "run": int(os.path.basename(f)[4:7]),
        "F_mean": F[steady].mean(),
        "F_std": F[steady].std(),
    })

pd.DataFrame(rows).to_csv("processed/forces_b3.csv", index=False)
